Scope remarks: almost entirely wire/type contracts, so rows are judged at the underlying capability level; TurboSpark's in-process design makes the transport/serialization layers genuinely unnecessary rather than a gap. The truly absent capabilities are the browser/computer-use protocol, the dynamic workflow engine, and cloud/remote service families.

Scope: the HTTP/WebSocket backend (incl. src/remote) and the standalone server runner
(supervisor, packaging, IPC, server-core).

Missing by design (no web client). |
| Shared-token auth for server endpoints (single token via env, query token exchanged for HttpOnly cookie, protects /ws and /api/*) | packages/server/src/http.ts | ../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Server/ServerKeychain.swift; ../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Server/ServerAPIKeyGenerator.swift | Partial | Both gate an HTTP surface with one shared secret; TurboSpark adds Keychain storage, an sk- key generator, x-api-key/Bearer headers and constant-time compare (crates/server/src/auth.rs). Zcode's token comes only from an env var with no secure store or generator. |
| One-time trusted-host capability tickets (30s single-use token to upgrade the host connection role; web clients locked to a lesser role) | packages/server/src/hostCapability.ts; packages/server/src/http.ts | none | Missing | TurboSpark's server has a single flat API key with no role separation; a single-user local app has less need for privilege tiers. |

| WSL and Docker remote backends (distro/container detection, UNC path handling, host-gateway proxy resolution, docker exec/upload) | packages/server/src/remote/wsl-backend.ts; packages/server/src/remote/docker-backend.ts | none | Missing | Same by-design gap; TurboSpark has no WSL/Docker target concepts. |

| Standalone server-core process (standalone authority mode, graceful shutdown with WebSocket drain, parent-disconnect shutdown) | packages/zcode-server-cli/src/server-core/core.ts; packages/zcode-server-cli/src/server-core/parentDisconnect.ts | ../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Server/ServerDaemonLaunch.swift | Partial | Both run the server as an independent process with its own bind/port, but TurboSpark's server binary is launched by the app rather than a supervisor, and graceful-drain/parent-disconnect semantics are not modeled. |

| Remote bundle build infrastructure (esbuild bundle keeping .node addons external, embedded provider config, bundle marker validation) | packages/server/build-remote.ts; packages/server/buildRemoteValidation.ts | none | Missing | Internal build plumbing for the deployable server artifact; only matters alongside remote deployment. |

Scope remarks: packages/server is mostly the remote-workspace and web-serving layer, which is Missing by design in single-machine TurboSpark; its closest analogs are the shared-token auth model and the standalone server process of TurboSpark's embedded server. packages/zcode-server-cli is process-lifecycle infrastructure (supervision, service registration, transactional updates) where TurboSpark only has the menu-bar daemon start/stop and Sparkle app updates, hence the several Partial rows.

Scope remarks: mostly headless infrastructure (rpc, client, provider, provider-node, model-option-map) plus web/cloud surfaces Zcode has and TurboSpark deliberately does not. The rows with genuine parity lessons for TurboSpark are the config overlay/ordering, legacy reasoning level renames, per-model option maps, and the fail-closed TCC-style permission contract patterns.


## 21. Build and packaging scripts, scripts/

Scope: `scripts/` at the Zcode repo root - build, packaging, distribution, and developer
infrastructure. TurboSpark build paths in this table are repo-root relative.

| Feature | Zcode source | TurboSpark source | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Repo bootstrap + pinned toolchain (one command sets up Node via mise pin, pnpm install, hooks) | scripts/bootstrap.mjs; scripts/mise-run.mjs | [Makefile](../Makefile), [rust-toolchain.toml](../rust-toolchain.toml) | Partial | [Makefile](../Makefile) targets plus rust-toolchain.toml pin the Rust toolchain, but there is no single bootstrap entry; a `make bootstrap` target wrapping toolchain check plus first build would improve it. |
| Safe workspace clean (removes only build artifacts with path assertions) | scripts/clean.mjs | [Makefile](../Makefile) (clean-cargo, clean-swift, clean-dist, clean-tmp) | Full | Equivalent capability; make clean is more granular per artifact type. |
| Desktop dev environment launcher (Electron desktop in test/production env, remote-prod CDN simulation) | scripts/dev-desktop-env.mjs; scripts/dev-desktop-remote-prod.mjs | [Makefile](../Makefile) (swift-app, swift-demo) | Partial | Native app runs via debug/release split; no test/production env matrix because there is no remote backend. |
| Desktop agent build orchestration (ordered workspace builds, artifact verification, staging into bundled-agents) | scripts/build-desktop-agent-cli.mjs; scripts/spawn-command.mjs | [Makefile](../Makefile) (swift-app) | Missing | No separate agent artifact to build; the agent is compiled into TurboSpark.app. |
| Desktop agent V8 bytecode compilation (bundle compiled to V8 cached data, hash-named immutable artifacts) | scripts/build-desktop-agent-bytecode.mjs; scripts/compile-desktop-agent-bytecode.cjs | none | Missing | Unneeded for a native app: Swift compiles to machine code and hides source the same way. |
| CLI distribution packaging (stages web/server/agent plus runtime deps into a versioned tarball; generates sha256.txt, latest.json, install.sh) | scripts/build-zcode.mjs; scripts/zcode-distribution/installer.mjs; scripts/zcode-distribution/assets.mjs | [scripts/make-app-bundle.sh](../scripts/make-app-bundle.sh), [.github/workflows/release.yml](../.github/workflows/release.yml) | Missing | TurboSpark instead ships a signed DMG plus Homebrew casks and a CLI zip via the release workflow; an install.sh tarball flow only makes sense for a Node runtime product. |
| Shipped launcher entry (bin/zcode.mjs starts web server + agent with token auth, port picking, browser open) | scripts/zcode-distribution/runner.mjs | Sources/TurboSparkApp/Server/ServerDaemonLaunch.swift | Missing | TurboSpark's server is started in-process from the app menu, not via a CLI launcher script. |
| Out-of-repo release smoke test (unpacks tarball to temp dir, runs help/version/runtime imports, web server) | scripts/zcode-distribution-smoke.mjs | [scripts/make-dmg.sh](../scripts/make-dmg.sh), [docs/RELEASE.md](RELEASE.md) | Partial | make-dmg.sh mounts the finished DMG and verifies resource bundle and signature before shipping, covering the same packaged-artifact-works concern. |
| Deterministic tar.gz archiver (hand-rolled ustar with fixed metadata, gzip mtime 0, reproducible archives) | scripts/deterministic-tar-archive.mjs | none | Missing | No reproducible-archive requirement; DMG and zip release assets are not byte-reproducible; low value for a signed/notarized app. |
| Native search tools source build (compiles bfs, ugrep, ripgrep from pinned, sha256-verified source archives) | scripts/build-native-search-tools.mjs; scripts/native-search-tools-config.mjs; scripts/native-search-tools-unix.mjs | ../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Tools/File/FileSearchTools.swift | Missing | TurboSpark implements Glob/Grep natively in Swift, so no external binaries are needed; bundling bfs/ugrep/rg would only pay off if native search underperforms on large trees. |
| Native search prebuilt fetch/verify/cache (downloads prebuilt tool releases, sha256 + Mach-O/ELF header verification) | scripts/prepare-native-search-tools.mjs; scripts/prebuilt-binary-extract.mjs; scripts/native-search-tools-bundle-meta.mjs | none | Missing | Native Swift search needs no binary distribution pipeline. |
| Windows MSVC/CMake cross-build and PE verification (builds static ugrep for win32, verifies PE imports) | scripts/native-search-tools-windows.mjs; scripts/native-search-tools-windows-pe.mjs | none | Missing | TurboSpark is deliberately arm64 macOS only ([scripts/swift-lib.sh](../scripts/swift-lib.sh) states ARM64 ONLY); Windows support would require a full port. |
| macOS release app doctor (validates an installed /Applications app via codesign --deep --strict, spctl, bundle identity) | scripts/doctor-macos-release-app.sh | [scripts/make-dmg.sh](../scripts/make-dmg.sh), [docs/RELEASE.md](RELEASE.md) | Partial | TurboSpark verifies signature and bundle contents at DMG build time, but has no doctor for an already-installed app; a small post-install check script would improve it. |
| Third-party notices generation + license gate (builds THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md from dependency tree with sha256-verified inventory) | scripts/generate-third-party-notices.mjs; scripts/third-party-npm.mjs; scripts/licenses.mjs | [NOTICE](../NOTICE), [docs/RELEASE.md](RELEASE.md) | Partial | NOTICE is a small hand-maintained provenance list; could be improved by generating notices from Cargo.lock and Package.resolved (cargo-about or similar). |
| Vendored SPDX license text corpus (14 canonical license texts consumed by the notices generator) | scripts/license-texts/ | none | Missing | Would come for free with a generated-notices pipeline; unnecessary with a hand-maintained NOTICE. |
| Release changelog automation (release-it with conventional-changelog preset, custom writer) | scripts/release-it/changelog-writer.mjs | [CHANGELOG.md](../CHANGELOG.md), [docs/RELEASE.md](RELEASE.md) | Partial | RELEASE.md documents a disciplined manual [CHANGELOG.md](../CHANGELOG.md) move per release; conventional-commit automation could improve it, though the manual process is intentionally reviewed. |
| Architecture policy checks (module/layer/dependency policy YAML, import resolution, cycle detection, violation baseline, pre-push hook) | scripts/architecture/architecture-check.mjs; scripts/architecture/policy.mjs | [Makefile](../Makefile) (catalog-guard) | Missing | catalog-guard tests catalog behavior, not dependency rules; periphery/SwiftLint custom rules or a test asserting crate and SwiftPM module boundaries would replicate it. |
| Dependency graph and symbol reference tools (mermaid graph of workspace imports, cross-references) | scripts/dependency-graph.mjs; scripts/dep-refs.mjs | none | Missing | A SwiftPM/crate graph dumper; low priority given the clear layering. |
| Pre-session workspace freshness gate (fails if branch is behind upstream beyond a threshold) | scripts/check-workspace-freshness.mjs | none | Missing | A `make preflight` target running a git ahead/behind check would replicate it. |
| Source line-count lint (flags .ts files over 400 lines) | scripts/count-lines.sh | none | Missing | A one-line wc/awk addition to the Makefile would replicate it for Swift sources. |
| Runtime prebuilds / asset producer (stages runtime releases, packs component sources deterministically, mock CDN mirror) | scripts/prepare-prebuilds.mjs; scripts/prepare-prebuilds.sh; scripts/intranetDefaults.mjs | [scripts/swift-lib.sh](../scripts/swift-lib.sh) | Partial | Approaches are inverted: TurboSpark builds its single native dependency (Rust staticlib) from source and copies it into SwiftPM, with no remote runtime assets to mirror. |
| Built-in provider build config (resolves env, stages provider config JSON next to the agent bundle) | scripts/builtin-provider-config.mjs | none | Missing | TurboSpark has exactly one local engine and no provider environment matrix. |

Scope remarks: almost entirely build, packaging, and developer-infrastructure tooling, so parity is mostly Missing by design rather than by product gap. Where TurboSpark has a counterpart (clean targets, DMG self-verification, NOTICE provenance, changelog discipline, from-source native builds) the native approach is simpler because there is one target platform, one local engine, and no Node runtime or remote asset distribution.


---

# Part B: Unsloth Desktop (unslothai/unsloth `studio/`) vs TurboSpark Swift app

Unsloth Desktop is the desktop product shipped from the unsloth repo. The product surface is the
`studio/` directory: a Tauri 2 Rust shell (`studio/src-tauri`) that owns process lifecycle, updates,
and native integrations; a Python backend (`studio/backend`, FastAPI-style router modules under
`routes/`, engine cores under `core/`) that runs inference (llama.cpp, MLX, MXC, NPU, lemonade,
diffusion, STT, video), training (LoRA and full fine-tune over the `unsloth` package), a local model
hub, RAG, and data tooling; and a React/TypeScript frontend (`studio/frontend`). The repo also
contains the `unsloth/` training library, a `unsloth` CLI, installers, docker images, and tests;
those passes cover the parts that surface in the desktop product. TurboSpark side: the Swift app
`swift/TurboSparkApp` only, as in Part A.

Method: every folder and file group of the unsloth checkout was assigned to one of 20 analysis
passes. Model-default config YAMLs, per-family configs, and vendored third-party bundles are
compared as grouped rows linking representative sources.

Results across N compared features: F Full, P Partial, M Missing (filled in at the end of the
pass series). Headline gaps expected by product shape: TurboSpark has no training, no NVIDIA/CUDA
path, no STT/audio-generation models, no video generation, no RAG, no local model-hub server, and
no account/auth cloud layer; Unsloth Desktop has no coding agent, no hooks, no MCP client of its
own at agent level, no profile vault, and ships one frontend stack per OS instead of a native
SwiftUI shell.

Contents (Part B):

1. [Tauri desktop shell, studio/src-tauri](#b1-tauri-desktop-shell-studiosrc-tauri)
2. [Backend app frame: main, auth, state, storage, loggers, tunnel, colab](#b2-backend-app-frame)
3. [HTTP routes, studio/backend/routes](#b3-http-routes-studiobackendroutes)
4. [Inference core part 1: chat pipeline, tools, MCP, skills, sandbox](#b4-inference-core-part-1-chat-pipeline)
5. [Inference core part 2: engines (llama.cpp, MLX, MXC, NPU, lemonade, offload, media switching)](#b5-inference-core-part-2-engines)
6. [Inference core part 3: audio and STT](#b6-inference-core-part-3-audio-and-stt)
7. [Inference core part 4: diffusion and image generation](#b7-inference-core-part-4-diffusion-and-image)
8. [Inference core part 5: video generation](#b8-inference-core-part-5-video)

10. [RAG, research, system one, picker, library, YouTube, tool healing](#b10-rag-research-systemone-picker-library)
11. [Hub service, models, utils, integrations, plugins](#b11-hub-models-utils-integrations-plugins)
12. [Backend assets: chat templates, model defaults, datasets, docs UI](#b12-backend-assets)
13. [Frontend shell, stores, hooks, i18n, shared components](#b13-frontend-shell-and-shared)

## B1. Tauri desktop shell, studio/src-tauri

Scope: all Rust shell modules (main, commands, app layout and menus, backend lifecycle, preflight,
install/update machinery, native integrations, diagnostics) plus Tauri packaging configs, NSIS and
Linux packaging.

| Feature | Unsloth source | TurboSpark source | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [Single-instance focus and Dock reopen](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/main.rs): second app launch focuses the existing window via the single-instance plugin, and a macOS Reopen event re-shows a hidden main window | [main.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/main.rs) | [TurboSparkApp.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/App/TurboSparkApp.swift) | Partial | TS relies on default macOS activation plus applicationShouldHandleReopen to reopen the window; there is no guard against `open -n` multi-instance launches. A one-line check (NSRunningApplication or LSMultipleInstancesProhibited) would close the gap. |
| [Launch at login with tamper-proof preference](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/main.rs): autostart plugin registers a `--hidden` login entry while the user's answer lives in a preference file that survives registry cleaners and antivirus deletes | [main.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/main.rs) | none | Missing | No SMAppService or launchd registration exists anywhere in the Swift app. Implementing means SMAppService (macOS 13+) plus a UserDefaults-backed answer kept beside the OS entry so external tampering cannot silently flip it, mirroring the launch-at-login-v1 file design. |
| [Close-to-tray and serialized quit reap](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/main.rs): the close button hides to tray or quits per preference, and exactly one exit path reaps installer, updater and backend children while the others block | [main.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/main.rs) | [TurboSparkApp.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/App/TurboSparkApp.swift), [MacAppSettings.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/MacAppSettings.swift) | Partial | applicationShouldTerminateAfterLastWindowClosed keeps the app resident when keepServerRunningInBackground or the menu bar item is on (default on), but there is no hide-vs-quit choice on window close and no single serialized cleanup path guaranteeing child shutdown ordering on quit. |
| [Menu bar tray icon with server status](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/macos_tray.rs): tray icon whose menu exposes open, quit and live server status, with a macOS appearance observer keeping the status-bar icon adaptive in dark and light mode | [macos_tray.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/macos_tray.rs), [main.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/main.rs) | [TurboSparkApp.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/App/TurboSparkApp.swift), [ServerMenuBarView.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Server/ServerMenuBarView.swift) | Full | TS has a toggleable MenuBarExtra (window style) rendering ServerMenuDashboardView with start/stop/restart daemon, endpoints and live stats, and the bolt icon reflects server state; SF Symbol templates adapt to appearance automatically. Difference: it is a menu bar extra rather than an NSStatusItem menu with Open/Quit, and it is not coupled to a hide-on-close mode. |
| [Rotating file logging](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/main.rs): combined terminal and file logger whose RotatingLogFile keeps exactly one generation and rotates on a byte threshold mid-session | [main.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/main.rs) | none | Missing | The Swift app writes no on-disk app log (only the spawned server daemon surfaces a logPath via TurboSparkDaemon.status). Implementing means os.Logger plus a size-capped rotating file, keeping one prior generation like the tauri.log.1 scheme. |
| [Window layout persistence and restore guards](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/app_layout.rs): size, position and maximized state restore via the window-state plugin, with init markers so the 760x560 setup size cannot clobber a saved layout and reset/has-initialized commands | [app_layout.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/app_layout.rs) | [TurboSparkApp.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/App/TurboSparkApp.swift), [MainWindowMinimumSize.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/App/MainWindowMinimumSize.swift) | Partial | TS does a one-time maximized-frame adoption and then macOS automatic frame autosave/restore, and MainWindowMinimumSize clamps restored frames; missing are the explicit initialized/reset marker machinery, restore-while-hidden ordering, and a user-facing reset command. |
| [Native macOS app menus](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/app_menu.rs): File, View, Go and Help menus built natively, actions dispatched as events the renderer enables contextually, Help search enumerating workspaces and settings pages | [app_menu.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/app_menu.rs) | [TurboSparkApp.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/App/TurboSparkApp.swift) | Full | TS declares native CommandMenu/CommandGroup menus (View, Chat, Generation, Model, Profile, Appearance) with accelerators directly in SwiftUI; no cross-process dispatch layer is needed because actions run in-process. |
| [Backend lifecycle and health watchdog commands](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/commands.rs): start/stop managed server, health probes distinguishing alive, warming-up and stalled, a watchdog that emits server-crashed events, backend presence checks, buffered logs and open-logs/models-dir commands | [commands.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/commands.rs) | [ServerMenuBarView.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Server/ServerMenuBarView.swift), [AppModel+Server.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/AppModel+Server.swift) | Partial | TS start/stop/restart the daemon with error-checked toasts, poll TurboSparkDaemon.status, and derive honest reported status rows (address, auth, guardrails); there is no crash watchdog with liveness-vs-stall classification emitting events, and no managed repair command. |
| [Managed backend process environment, spawn and stop](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/process.rs): spawns the backend with scrubbed environment (AppImage LD_LIBRARY_PATH, GUI PATH fix via login shell), keeps a 1000-line log ring, and stops with graceful-then-force escalation | [process.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/process.rs) | [ServerDaemonLaunch.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Server/ServerDaemonLaunch.swift) | Partial | ServerDaemonLaunch builds daemon args purely and moves the API key into the child environment so it stays off ps, but TS has no equivalent env scrubbing for host library paths, no login-shell PATH repair for GUI launches, and no ring-buffer capture of child output (the daemon logs to its own file). |
| [Desktop auth secret exchange](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/desktop_auth.rs): provisions a .desktop_secret and exchanges it for access and refresh tokens over a loopback client that refuses proxies and redirects, retrying across discovered ports and recording auth failures | [desktop_auth.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/desktop_auth.rs), [loopback_http.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/loopback_http.rs) | [ServerKeychain.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Server/ServerKeychain.swift) | Missing | No account or login flow exists; the closest verified analog is keychain storage of the local server API key. Implementing would mean a secret provisioning handshake with a backend, a redirect-proof loopback token exchange, and Keychain-backed token storage with refresh. |
| [Backend ownership, adoption and process identity](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/desktop_backend_owner.rs): an owner token, pid and kind env contract lets the app adopt a live backend across restarts without killing a possibly mid-training process, and process_identity tells which install tree a pid runs from | [desktop_backend_owner.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/desktop_backend_owner.rs), [process_identity.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/process_identity.rs) | none | Missing | The engine runs in-process so there is no external backend to own or adopt; sessions persist via AppModel persistence instead. Implementing adoption for the daemon would need an owner-token handshake, pid record files, and executable-path identity checks. |
| [Startup preflight dispositions](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/preflight.rs): classifies startup as NotInstalled, ManagedReady, ManagedStale, OwnedReady/Stale or AttachedReady using a versioned managed capability cache (schema 4), pid records and protocol/manageability versions that drive repair | [preflight.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/preflight.rs), [types.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/preflight/types.rs), [backend.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/preflight/backend.rs), [managed.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/preflight/managed.rs), [pid_records.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/preflight/pid_records.rs), [version.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/preflight/version.rs) | [ModelInstallGate.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Installation/ModelInstallGate.swift), [ModelProbeGating.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Installation/ModelProbeGating.swift) | Partial | TS gates model installs (allowed/confirm/blocked from hardware fit, disk, engine memory ladder) and sanitizes probe repo input, but these gate checkpoints, not app or runtime install state; there is no cached capability schema, pid record, or versioned repair contract for the app shell itself. |
| [First-run managed install with elevation](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/install.rs): streams installer script progress events, parses a NEEDS_ELEVATION marker into an explicit package allowlist for an approval-gated elevated install, and supports repair resume | [install.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/install.rs) | [ModelHubView.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Installation/ModelHubView.swift), [ModelDownload.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/ModelDownload.swift) | Partial | TS streams model installs with progress, transfer meter and ETA and gates before start, but there is no script-driven managed runtime install and no elevation handshake (macOS needs no system packages); an equivalent would only matter if the app gained a companion runtime to provision. |
| [Install watchdog](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/install_watchdog.rs): a 12 hour backstop with 5 minute progress reports keeps slow installs alive and states what the install is waiting on, without silence-based kills that waste downloads | [install_watchdog.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/install_watchdog.rs) | [AppModel+Installation.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/AppModel+Installation.swift) | Partial | TS computes an honest install ETA with an elapsed-time floor, but nothing bounds pathological installs or reports what a long download or install is waiting on; a watchdog with a backstop and periodic phase reporting would carry over directly. |
| [Managed backend update and staged cleanup](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/update.rs): runs the backend's own update command with streamed progress and attempt logs, and reconciles leftover .update-stage/.update-prev trees from older staged updates at launch | [update.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/update.rs), [staged_update.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/staged_update.rs) | none | Missing | The engine is embedded in the TS app, so engine updates ride the Sparkle app update and no separate managed-runtime update or legacy stage cleanup path exists; an equivalent would appear only if TS adopted an out-of-process runtime with its own update channel. |
| [In-app signed desktop self-update](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/desktop_updater.rs): checks a signed manifest, verifies bundle signatures against the minisign pubkey in tauri.conf.json, downloads and installs with an armed cleanup that survives interruption, with per-OS policy (in-app updater vs manual Linux package) | [desktop_updater.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/desktop_updater.rs), [desktop_update_policy.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/desktop_update_policy.rs) | [SparkleUpdateController.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/App/Updates/SparkleUpdateController.swift) | Full | TS uses Sparkle 2 with an EdDSA-signed appcast served from GitHub releases, automatic and manual checks, a settings toggle, and a silent AutoInstallUserDriver proven by an unattended two-version smoke; macOS-only scope means the Linux manual-package policy branch has no counterpart. |
| [Debian in-place package update](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/debian_update.rs): deb-bundled app re-executes the installed binary with an install argument to upgrade the system package in place under the polkit action, with a 512 MB package cap | [debian_update.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/debian_update.rs) | none | Missing | N/A for a macOS-only app; a package-manager update path would only become relevant if TS shipped a Linux distribution, in which case a polkit-gated updater and a packaged entry point would be needed. |
| [Windows job object child containment](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/windows_job.rs): assigns the app to a kill-on-close job object so every child process dies with the app even on abnormal termination | [windows_job.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/windows_job.rs) | none | Missing | No equivalent; the turbospark-server daemon is stopped only through TurboSparkDaemon.stop on user action, and nothing OS-enforced ties the child's lifetime to the app on crash or force quit. N/A while macOS-only, but relevant for daemon containment hardening. |
| [Linux display server compatibility](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/x11_threads.rs): enables Xlib thread locking before any X use and selects WebKitGTK rendering fallbacks (shared memory instead of dmabuf on NVIDIA, GLES handling in AppImage, Wayland vs X11 detection) | [x11_threads.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/x11_threads.rs), [linux_webkit.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/linux_webkit.rs) | none | Missing | N/A while macOS-only (WKWebView has no analogous renderer selection); recorded as a cross-platform portability gap rather than a functional one. |
| [Webview microphone permission reset](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/webview_permissions.rs): clears WebView2's saved deny decision so the next getUserMedia prompts again, fixing a Windows-only permanent dictation block | [webview_permissions.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/webview_permissions.rs) | [SystemPermissionsManager.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/SystemPermissionsManager.swift) | Missing | TS has no microphone feature at all (no dictation or STT), so there is nothing to reset; SystemPermissionsManager tracks protected-folder access only. Adding voice input would require the mic usage-description plist entry, a permission prompt flow, and a reset path. |
| [Support diagnostics collection](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/diagnostics/mod.rs): collects a support report from a phase log, redaction layer, diagnostics state and auth failure records so users can hand over one artifact | [mod.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/diagnostics/mod.rs), [phase_log.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/diagnostics/phase_log.rs), [redaction.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/diagnostics/redaction.rs), [report.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/diagnostics/report.rs), [state.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/diagnostics/state.rs) | [InspectorView.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Diagnostics/InspectorView.swift), [AppDiagnostics.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/AppDiagnostics.swift) | Partial | TS has a rich in-app Inspector (model, memory/power, sampling, runner, server telemetry, usage dashboard) and per-generation metric capture, but no one-click redacted support report or bundle export; adding an export that gathers settings-safe state, daemon log path and recent metrics would close it. |
| [Native clipboard file and image intake](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/native_clipboard.rs): reads clipboard file lists, PNG images and audio with format sniffing and strict size caps so chat can attach what the OS clipboard holds | [native_clipboard.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/native_clipboard.rs) | none | Missing | Pasteboard use in the Swift app is write-only (text copy actions) and composer paste handles text only. Implementing means NSPasteboard reads of file URLs, images and audio with type sniffing, dimension and byte caps, feeding the existing attachment chip pipeline. |
| [Native file dialogs and chunked import](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/native_file_dialogs.rs): native save and pick dialogs for exports, chat imports (streamed to the renderer in 8 MB chunks), training configs, and logs-to-Downloads, with default-name headers | [native_file_dialogs.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/native_file_dialogs.rs) | [PromptComposerView.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Generation/PromptComposerView.swift), [AppChatExport.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/AppChatExport.swift) | Partial | TS uses native panels directly (fileImporter in the composer, NSOpenPanel/NSSavePanel across chat export, model location pick and profile backup), and in-process reads need no chunking, but there is no chat-import counterpart to its chat export and no save-logs-to-Downloads action. |
| [Drag-drop intake and path authorization](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/native_intents.rs): records only paths the OS actually dropped (with a grace window), issues short-TTL path tokens and HMAC-signed path leases for model, dataset and attachment operations, and reveals or opens targets in the file manager | [native_intents.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/native_intents.rs), [native_path_policy.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/native_path_policy.rs), [native_backend_lease.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/native_backend_lease.rs) | [PathContainment.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/PathContainment.swift), [AppModel+Files.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/AppModel+Files.swift) | Partial | TS has canonicalized symlink-containment policy for untrusted project files and reveal-in-Finder across model, server and file surfaces, but the composer has no drop intake (file pickers only) and no signed path tokens; the in-process design removes the token need, while a dropDestination intake on the composer is the concrete missing piece. |
| [Deep-link URL scheme](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/main.rs): registers the unsloth:// scheme (Windows/Linux registration at setup, desktop entry MimeType on Linux) so OS-level links and login callbacks open the app | [main.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/src/main.rs), [tauri.conf.json](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/tauri.conf.json) | none | Missing | No CFBundleURLTypes declaration and no onOpenURL handler exist in the Swift app. Implementing means declaring the scheme in the bundle and routing onOpenURL events into an intent queue with the same single-flight semantics. |
| [Declarative packaging and security config](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/tauri.conf.json): one config drives CSP with HF mirror allowlisting, a per-window IPC capability allowlist, sandbox entitlements and mic usage string, updater artifacts, per-OS bundle overrides, icons and dmg branding | [tauri.conf.json](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/tauri.conf.json), [tauri.macos.conf.json](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/tauri.macos.conf.json), [tauri.windows.conf.json](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/tauri.windows.conf.json), [tauri.linux.conf.json](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/tauri.linux.conf.json), [default.json](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/capabilities/default.json), [Entitlements.plist](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/Entitlements.plist), [Info.plist](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/Info.plist), [build.rs](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/build.rs), [Cargo.toml](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/Cargo.toml), [background.tiff](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/dmg/background.tiff) | [Package.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Package.swift) | Partial | The SwiftPM manifest defines the app build and SparkleUpdateController documents an ad-hoc signed bundle trusting the Sparkle EdDSA key, but the app target has no sandbox entitlements, no IPC/permission allowlist, no notarization config, and no per-OS bundle overrides; hardened-runtime entitlements plus a notarized dmg flow would close most of the gap. |
| [Custom Windows NSIS installer](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/windows/installer.nsi): a pinned, patched NSIS template handling in-place upgrades from non-WiX versions, WebView2 bootstrapper embedding, signed plugin directories, plus hooks that clean legacy files and preserve shared user data on uninstall | [installer.nsi](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/windows/installer.nsi), [hooks.nsh](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/windows/hooks.nsh) | none | Missing | TS installs by drag-to-Applications dmg delivered through Sparkle, with no installer stage at all; a real installer (PKG or NSIS on a Windows port) would need upgrade-in-place handling, signed helper components and uninstall preservation rules like these hooks. |
| [Linux desktop integration packaging](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/linux/unsloth.desktop): a desktop entry with deep-link MimeType handling, a polkit action gating updates, AppImage AppRun scripts that scrub the host environment, and deb postremove scripts | [unsloth.desktop](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/linux/unsloth.desktop), [ai.unsloth.studio.update.policy](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/linux/ai.unsloth.studio.update.policy), [appimage-apprun.sh](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/linux/appimage-apprun.sh), [postremove.sh](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/linux/postremove.sh), [prepare-complete-appimage-tools.sh](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/linux/prepare-complete-appimage-tools.sh), [finalize-complete-appimage.sh](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/linux/finalize-complete-appimage.sh), [verify-complete-appimage.sh](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/linux/verify-complete-appimage.sh), [appimage-fonts.conf](../../unsloth/studio/src-tauri/linux/appimage-fonts.conf) | none | Missing | N/A while TS is macOS-only; a Linux distribution would need a desktop entry with URL handling, a polkit policy for privileged operations, and AppRun environment scrubbing equivalent to what process.rs and these scripts implement. |

## B2. Backend app frame

Scope: `studio/backend` top-level app (main, launcher, LAN access, tunnel, colab, MCP server
exposure), auth stack, run-state registries, loggers, storage suite, vendored truststore, and the
shared installer/runtime plumbing plus the four studio-level capability docs.

| Feature | Unsloth source | TurboSpark source | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [FastAPI backend app frame](../../unsloth/studio/backend/main.py): FastAPI app with lifespan hardware detection and torch warmup, middleware stack (logging, nonced CSP security headers, body-size limits, keep-warm, remote-access CORS), /api/health plus /v1 OpenAI-compatible routes, and static SPA hosting with immutable cached assets | [main.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/main.py) | [AppModel+Server.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/AppModel+Server.swift) | Partial | TS embeds a Rust server (route families in ServerEndpointCatalog) instead of a Python web app and serves no browser frontend from it; the gap is web hosting plus CSP and body-limit hardening, which would mean adding static hosting and header middleware to crates/server if a web panel is ever wanted. |
| [Launcher and process lifecycle](../../unsloth/studio/backend/run.py): standalone launcher that finds a free port with bump notices, writes pid files with live-sibling detection, installs signal and Windows console shutdown handlers, tees stdout to a session disk log, reaps orphaned children, emits the TAURI_PORT line for the desktop shell, and prints the access banner | [run.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/run.py), [startup_banner.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/startup_banner.py), [__init__.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/__init__.py) | [ServerDaemonLaunch.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Server/ServerDaemonLaunch.swift), [ServerPortInput.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Server/ServerPortInput.swift) | Partial | TS covers OS-assigned ports (0 means automatic) and background daemon spawn with API-key hygiene and a meta file, but there is no live-sibling pid detection and the request console is in-memory only; a rotated on-disk server log beside the daemon meta file would preserve evidence after a crash. |
| [Import-time platform hardening](../../unsloth/studio/backend/main.py): pins CUDA_DEVICE_ORDER, imports ROCm env from the login shell the desktop shell dropped, reconfigures Windows streams to UTF-8, registers ROCm DLL directories, and repairs bitsandbytes ROCm DLL detection before torch loads | [main.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/main.py) | none | Missing | Most of this is not applicable to a native Metal app, but the analog exists at spawn sites; if TS ever spawns tools needing login-shell PATH or SDK env, replicate the import-from-login-shell step where AgentTerminalLaunch and ServerDaemonLaunch build child environments. |
| [Multi-account auth stack](../../unsloth/studio/backend/auth/authentication.py): JWT access and refresh sessions, bearer API-key auth with PBKDF2-hashed keys and timing equalization, single versus multi-user policy cache, and SQLite credentials with rotation fences and bootstrap password files | [authentication.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/auth/authentication.py), [storage.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/auth/storage.py), [hashing.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/auth/hashing.py), [policy.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/auth/policy.py) | [ServerKeychain.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Server/ServerKeychain.swift), [ServerAdvancedSettingsView.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Server/ServerAdvancedSettingsView.swift) | Partial | TS has one static server API key stored in the Keychain and required on every route, with a warning row when absent, but no login, sessions, refresh tokens, or multi-user scoping; hashed, revocable per-client keys would be the concrete improvement. |
| [Bootstrap password gate and auto-shutdown](../../unsloth/studio/backend/auth/terminal_prompt.py): forces a first-run admin password change on the terminal before an exposed instance becomes reachable, and tears the server down after a deadline if the seeded password is never changed | [terminal_prompt.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/auth/terminal_prompt.py), [bootstrap_timeout.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/auth/bootstrap_timeout.py) | none | Missing | TS can start its server with no key (the UI only colors the auth row as a warning); implementing this would mean refusing to serve or auto-stopping after a deadline when the Keychain key is empty and the bind is not loopback-only. |
| [Runtime LAN access listener](../../unsloth/studio/backend/lan_access.py): adds a second live uvicorn listener on the machine's LAN addresses over the already-running app, no restart needed, with WSL-aware address discovery and drain-trust semantics | [lan_access.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/lan_access.py) | none | Missing | TS is loopback-only (host field defaults to 127.0.0.1); a LAN toggle would mean an additional NWListener bound to detected en0 addresses reusing the same API key, plus a settings pane row and reachability display. |
| [Cloudflare quick tunnel](../../unsloth/studio/backend/cloudflare_tunnel.py): finds or downloads cloudflared, opens a free trycloudflare.com HTTPS URL, verifies DNS and /api/health through the edge, and publishes or stops the URL live via callbacks | [cloudflare_tunnel.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/cloudflare_tunnel.py) | none | Missing | No tunnel support anywhere in the Swift app; implementing it would mean bundling or fetching a tunnel binary, spawning it as a child, parsing the URL, and gating the published endpoint behind the API key requirement. |
| [Colab notebook integration](../../unsloth/studio/backend/colab.py): detects a Colab runtime, resolves the kernel proxy URL with retries, embeds the UI in an iframe, shows a ready card, and persists Colab login credentials | [colab.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/colab.py) | none | Missing | TS is a native Mac app with no hosted-notebook surface; the nearest equivalent would be documented guidance for driving the local server API from a remote notebook rather than an embedded UI. |
| [Own MCP server exposure](../../unsloth/studio/backend/mcp_server.py): opt-in FastMCP server mounted at /mcp exposing curated status, training, recipe, checkpoint-load, and GGUF-export tools behind an exact bearer token checked on HTTP and WebSocket | [mcp_server.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/mcp_server.py) | [McpClientEngine.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Tools/MCP/McpClientEngine.swift) | Partial | TS consumes MCP servers (JSON-RPC client engine plus external-agent config import) but never exposes one; a read-only status and discovery MCP endpoint over the local server would give external agents visibility without GPU-mutating tools. |
| [In-process run state registries](../../unsloth/studio/backend/state/active_generations.py): in-flight generation registry guarding reloads with 409s, per-account heartbeats tracking which durable runs a browser watches, a per-call tool-approval gate answered on a separate connection, and CLI tool-policy override and default slots | [active_generations.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/state/active_generations.py), [run_subscribers.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/state/run_subscribers.py), [tool_approvals.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/state/tool_approvals.py), [tool_policy.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/state/tool_policy.py) | [AppModel+Tools.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/AppModel+Tools.swift) | Partial | TS has approval cards with per-session always-allow persistence and cancellation, but approval state lives in the UI process only, so an HTTP API client cannot confirm tool calls; a server-side confirmation channel keyed by approval id would close that gap. |
| [Structured logging stack](../../unsloth/studio/backend/loggers/config.py): structlog configuration with JSON and console modes, context vars, and level filtering; request/response middleware with sensitive-data redaction; and compact media progress logging | [config.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/loggers/config.py), [handlers.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/loggers/handlers.py), [media_progress.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/loggers/media_progress.py) | [ServerConsoleView.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Server/ServerConsoleView.swift) | Partial | TS renders a strong in-memory request console (one row per request, expandable raw events) but no on-disk structured session log with redaction was found in the app tree; a rotating JSONL log would cover post-crash evidence. |
| [SQLite persistence suite for runs](../../unsloth/studio/backend/storage/studio_db.py): per-account studio.db holding training history and metrics plus transactional durable state and cursor events for chat generations and deep research runs | [studio_db.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/storage/studio_db.py), [chat_generation_runs_db.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/storage/chat_generation_runs_db.py), [research_runs_db.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/storage/research_runs_db.py) | [AppModel+Persistence.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/AppModel+Persistence.swift), [ProfileDatabase.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/ProfileDatabase.swift) | Partial | TS persists chats and settings as JSON and keeps per-profile SQLCipher databases, but resumable run-level state with cursors for server API clients is absent; a run journal table in the profile DB would let API clients resume after a crash. |
| [Usage receipts and profile stats](../../unsloth/studio/backend/storage/api_usage_db.py): durable content-free hashed receipts of external API usage plus derived per-profile usage statistics served to the UI | [api_usage_db.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/storage/api_usage_db.py), [profile_stats_db.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/storage/profile_stats_db.py) | [ServerMetricsStore.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Server/ServerMetricsStore.swift), [UsageDashboardSection.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Diagnostics/UsageDashboardSection.swift) | Partial | TS keeps per-request records with token counts in memory and a per-day usage ledger persisted with chats, rendered as a dashboard with heatmap and charts; durable hashed receipts for third-party API calls so history survives relaunch would close the gap. |
| [Encrypted credentials and provider registry](../../unsloth/studio/backend/storage/credential_secrets.py): encrypted install-wide credential persistence plus SQLite storage for external LLM provider configurations | [credential_secrets.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/storage/credential_secrets.py), [providers_db.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/storage/providers_db.py) | [ProfileVaultCrypto.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/ProfileVaultCrypto.swift), [ServerKeychain.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Server/ServerKeychain.swift) | Partial | TS encrypts profile data with PBKDF2 (600k rounds) and Keychain-backed master keys and holds the server key in the Keychain, but has no registry of external API providers since it is a local-inference app; adding one would mean a provider store plus Keychain-stored per-provider secrets. |
| [MCP server directory persistence](../../unsloth/studio/backend/storage/mcp_servers_db.py): SQLite-backed CRUD for the MCP servers the Studio UI manages | [mcp_servers_db.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/storage/mcp_servers_db.py) | [GlobalMcpFileStore.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/GlobalMcpFileStore.swift) | Full | TS persists global MCP server configs to a JSON archive in its storage root and adds an import wizard that reads other agents' configs (ExternalAgentMcpReader); file instead of SQLite is the only real difference at this scale. |
| [Library and RAG storage](../../unsloth/studio/backend/storage/library_db.py): SQLite stores for the document library and the RAG engine including chunk indexing | [library_db.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/storage/library_db.py), [rag_db.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/storage/rag_db.py) | none | Missing | TS has no document library or RAG index; implementing it would mean a chunk store plus an embeddings pipeline, likely backed by the existing per-profile SQLCipher database and the text extraction already present for attachments. |
| [Vendored truststore for OS-trust TLS](../../unsloth/studio/backend/vendor/README.md): byte-identical truststore 0.10.4 checked in with a sha256 manifest so TLS verifies against the OS trust store even behind a corporate inspecting proxy that would break downloading the package itself | [README.md](../../unsloth/studio/backend/vendor/README.md), [truststore_manifest.json](../../unsloth/studio/backend/vendor/truststore_manifest.json) | none | Full | Platform-inherent on TS: URLSession and the Security framework verify against the OS trust store by default, so the corporate-proxy problem truststore exists to solve does not arise and no vendoring is needed; the capability is present without a Swift source file. |
| [Install-completeness manifest](../../unsloth/studio/install_manifest.py): manifest written only after the dependency pass finishes, with cross-process locks, requirement digests, and metadata consistency checks, read by verify-install, the setup fast path, and the desktop preflight | [install_manifest.py](../../unsloth/studio/install_manifest.py) | [AppModel+Installation.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/AppModel+Installation.swift) | Partial | TS ships a compiled bundle and tracks checkpoint downloads with a verifying state and relaunch recovery, but nothing gates readiness on a persisted completeness manifest; recording per-asset sha256 manifests in ManagedAssetStore and gating model availability on them is the concrete analog. |
| [Prebuilt installer core](../../unsloth/studio/prebuilt_core.py): descriptor-driven machinery shared by the ggml-family prebuilt installers: verified downloads with retries and token-safe redirects, checksum indexes, CUDA runtime-line selection, staged atomic activation, and busy-lock conflict handling | [prebuilt_core.py](../../unsloth/studio/prebuilt_core.py) | [ModelDownload.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/ModelDownload.swift), [AppModel+Downloads.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/AppModel+Downloads.swift) | Partial | TS downloads and packs model checkpoints with pause, verify, and resume states, but never fetches engine binaries because Metal kernels ship in the app; the download layer would need the same staged-atomic and checksum-index rigor if external engine artifacts are ever added. |
| [NVIDIA library inventory probe](../../unsloth/studio/nvidia_probe.py): reads NVML or the CUDA driver API in a deadline-bounded child process, with a /proc/driver fallback, so a missing or hung nvidia-smi cannot misread a GPU host as CPU-only | [nvidia_probe.py](../../unsloth/studio/nvidia_probe.py) | none | Missing | TS is Metal-only and surfaces machine memory in the Inspector, and never queries a vendor accelerator library; if multi-vendor support lands, the pattern to copy is the fail-closed child probe with a deadline. |
| [MCP capability documentation](../../unsloth/studio/MCP.md): documents connecting the Blender MCP bridge (pinned checksum-verified runtime, loopback-only, existing tool permissions apply) and enabling Unsloth's own opt-in token-protected MCP server | [MCP.md](../../unsloth/studio/MCP.md) | [McpClientEngine.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Tools/MCP/McpClientEngine.swift), [McpServerSpec.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Tools/MCP/McpServerSpec.swift) | Partial | TS implements the client half (stdio and HTTP transports, spec forms, marketplace) but ships no exposed MCP server and no user-facing doc of one; either a read-only status endpoint or an explicit statement of why one is omitted would match the documented surface. |
| [Scanned PDF OCR documentation](../../unsloth/studio/PDF_OCR.md): documents scanned-page OCR for Chat, project sources, and Data Recipes using the loaded vision GGUF then local Tesseract, with language data setup, page budgets, DPI and language settings, and fail-closed uploads | [PDF_OCR.md](../../unsloth/studio/PDF_OCR.md) | [DocumentTextExtractor.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Presentation/DocumentTextExtractor.swift) | Partial | TS extracts text from PDF, DOCX, and XLSX attachments via PDFKit and the Office XML parsers, but image-only pages yield nothing; wiring Vision text recognition into DocumentTextExtractor for image pages with a page budget and explicit failure would close the gap. |
| [GPU P2P gating decision record](../../unsloth/studio/NVLINK_P2P.md): documents why GGML_CUDA_P2P is gated on confirmed NVLink topology, because IOMMU translating mode silently drops PCIe peer copies while CUDA reports success, so unknown means no | [NVLINK_P2P.md](../../unsloth/studio/NVLINK_P2P.md) | none | Missing | A single-GPU Metal app has no peer fabric today; the equivalent artifact would be a fail-closed decision record for any future multi-GPU Metal feature (for example Metal peer groups) stating when cross-device transfer is refused. |
| [RDNA2 APU routing record](../../unsloth/studio/ROCM_RDNA2_APU.md): measured findings that Vulkan beats ROCm for GGUF inference and ROCm training is numerically wrong on gfx1033 APUs, recorded so install routing is not reverted to ROCm | [ROCM_RDNA2_APU.md](../../unsloth/studio/ROCM_RDNA2_APU.md) | none | Missing | TS is Metal-first and needs no AMD routing, but it has no equivalent negative-finding record for Apple Silicon engine choices; a documented fail-closed decision (for example why a checkpoint family must not enable a given backend) would be the analog. |
| [Update and install-source status surface](../../unsloth/studio/backend/main.py): authenticated /api/studio endpoints the UI polls for install source, update availability, and version-gated release notes | [main.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/main.py) | [SparkleUpdateController.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/App/Updates/SparkleUpdateController.swift) | Full | TS uses Sparkle 2 with an EdDSA-signed GitHub Releases appcast, gated off bare swift-run builds; it is app-initiated rather than backend-polled and carries no release-notes API, which are the small differences. |

## B3. HTTP routes, studio/backend/routes

Scope: every route module of the Python backend, read as the user-facing desktop feature each
enables (browse/install models, inference control, serving APIs, chat history, training control,
RAG, research, skills, prompts, settings, providers, NPU, library, video, whisper, YouTube,
system one, preview).

| Feature | Unsloth source | TurboSpark source | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [Local model hub and management](../../unsloth/studio/backend/routes/models.py): browse and install models, custom scan folders, GGUF variants, download progress, cached model and LoRA listing, deletion, KV cache and memory estimates | [models.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/routes/models.py) | [ModelHubView.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Installation/ModelHubView.swift), [ModelStorageManager.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/ModelStorageManager.swift), [ModelDownload.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/ModelDownload.swift) | Full | Full for the hub flow: catalog browse, fit checking, installs with history, and custom external scan folders all exist. Small differences: no GGUF variant picker (installs are curated .gturbo packages) and no LoRA listing surface. |
| [Inference engine control](../../unsloth/studio/backend/routes/inference.py): load, validate, estimate memory, unload, status, and load progress for the resident model | [inference.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/routes/inference.py) | [AppModel+Installation.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/AppModel+Installation.swift), [ModelLoaderControl.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Chrome/ModelLoaderControl.swift) | Full | Full as an in-app feature: model load with honest ETA, progress, and unload live in the installation state and loader control. Difference: there is no HTTP control surface for it, only the app UI. |
| [OpenAI, Anthropic, and Ollama compatible serving API](../../unsloth/studio/backend/routes/inference.py): chat completions, completions, responses, embeddings, messages, count tokens, plus llama.cpp props and version shims | [inference.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/routes/inference.py), [llama_compat.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/routes/llama_compat.py) | [ServerEndpointCatalog.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Server/ServerEndpointCatalog.swift), [AppModel+Server.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/AppModel+Server.swift) | Partial | The embedded local server covers chat completions, completions, responses, embeddings, messages, count tokens, and the Ollama family. Gap: no OpenAI images or audio endpoints and no llama.cpp /props shim. Improvement: extend crates/server with /v1/images/generations and add the route to the catalog test. |
| [llama.cpp backend management](../../unsloth/studio/backend/routes/llama.py): backend status, self update with changelog, and switching between backends | [llama.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/routes/llama.py) | [EngineSettingsPaneView.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/App/EngineSettingsPaneView.swift) | Partial | The engine settings pane configures the engine, sampling, reasoning, guardrails, and the in-process server, but TurboSpark has one Metal engine. Gap: no alternative backend to switch to and no in-app self update or changelog flow; app updates go through Sparkle instead. |
| [Multi-account auth and accounts](../../unsloth/studio/backend/routes/auth.py): JWT login, refresh, desktop login, initial and change password, setup codes, rate limiting, and owner-managed account lifecycle | [auth.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/routes/auth.py), [accounts.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/routes/accounts.py) | [ProfileVault.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/ProfileVault.swift), [ProfilesSettingsPaneView.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Components/ProfilesSettingsPaneView.swift) | Partial | Local profiles with passphrase or LocalAuthentication protection and full create, rename, delete, switch, and backup cover the identity and locking story. Gap: no HTTP auth, tokens, multi-account server roots, or login throttling. Improvement: if the local server ever becomes remote reachable, add bearer token issuance backed by the existing keychain store. |

| [Chat attachments store](../../unsloth/studio/backend/routes/chat_history.py): per message attachment upload, file retrieval, and deletion | [chat_history.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/routes/chat_history.py) | [AttachmentImporter.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Files/AttachmentImporter.swift) | Partial | Attachments import into chats with extraction for PDF and office documents and render in previews. | [Resumable server-owned chat generation runs](../../unsloth/studio/backend/routes/chat_generation_runs.py): create runs, poll active, cancel, and replay SSE events with sensitive payload scrubbing | [chat_generation_runs.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/routes/chat_generation_runs.py) | [AppModel+Generation.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/AppModel+Generation.swift) | Partial | In-app streaming turns with cancellation and queueing exist, but a turn lives only in process. | [Dataset upload and format checks](../../unsloth/studio/backend/routes/datasets.py): upload datasets, list local datasets, download progress, format checks, and AI mapping assist | [datasets.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/routes/datasets.py) | none | Missing | Implementing would involve a dataset intake store, a chat format validator, and a mapping assistant in the Swift tree; TurboSpark has no fine-tuning data surface today, so it would start with a Files-adjacent dataset browser and validator. |
| [Data recipe dataset curation](../../unsloth/studio/backend/routes/data_recipe/jobs.py): recipe job lifecycle with status, cancel, analysis, dataset download, and SSE events, plus seed inspection of uploaded files, validation, and an MCP tool listing helper | [data_recipe/jobs.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/routes/data_recipe/jobs.py), [data_recipe/seed.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/routes/data_recipe/seed.py), [data_recipe/validate.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/routes/data_recipe/validate.py), [data_recipe/mcp.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/routes/data_recipe/mcp.py) | none | Missing | Implementing would involve long-running analysis jobs over uploaded corpora, unstructured file block inspection, and a validation pass; the nearest building blocks are the app file tools and attachment extractor, but no job framework for dataset curation exists. |

| [Training job control](../../unsloth/studio/backend/routes/training.py): hardware report, start requests, start, stop, reset, status, metrics, progress streaming, diffusion training jobs, and VRAM coordination with resident chat models | [training.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/routes/training.py), [training_vram.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/routes/training_vram.py) | none | Missing | Implementing would involve a training executor crate, job state machine, metrics stream, and memory arbitration against the resident decode model; none of this exists in the Swift app or crates today. |

## B4. Inference core part 1: chat pipeline

Scope: `studio/backend/core/inference` chat-side files: the generate loop and orchestrator, chat
templates and turn-end handling, context management and compaction, sampling and repetition, tool
call parsing and loop control, OS sandboxing, MCP client, skills, external providers, web tools,
pricing and monitoring.

| Feature | Unsloth source | TurboSpark source | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [Core local chat inference backend](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/inference.py): streaming generate loop over the local model with sampling, iterators and error formatting for the chat UI | [inference.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/inference.py) | [AppModel+Generation.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/AppModel+Generation.swift) | Full | TS drives its Rust/Metal engine through the same turn lifecycle (window fit, options, event stream, tool handoff); unsloth adds vision and audio generation paths the TS chat does not surface. |
| [Subprocess inference orchestrator and worker](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/orchestrator.py): persistent subprocess per model with load/unload/cancel commands and clean-env version switching | [orchestrator.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/orchestrator.py), [worker.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/worker.py) | [AppModel+ModelDiscovery.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/AppModel+ModelDiscovery.swift), [AppModel+Gating.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/AppModel+Gating.swift) | Partial | TS loads, switches and unloads models in-process over FFI with lifecycle gating but no out-of-process host; a subprocess model server would add crash isolation and per-model dependency isolation. |
| [Turn-end stop token resolution](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/chat_eos.py): derive each chat family's assistant turn-end markers from the chat template so generation stops at the turn, not the document terminator | [chat_eos.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/chat_eos.py) | [EngineSettingsPaneView.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/App/EngineSettingsPaneView.swift) | Partial | TS exposes user-set Stop Sequences but derives no per-family turn-end markers from the template; auto-deriving template turn-end tokens before a run would prevent run-on turns on quants with wrong eos. |
| [Chat template handling](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/chat_template_helpers.py): apply_chat_template with reasoning/tools kwarg fallbacks, bundled fresh templates for stale GGUF quants, and Jinja-level detection of whether a template renders the tool catalog | [chat_template_helpers.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/chat_template_helpers.py), [chat_templates.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/chat_templates.py), [template_capabilities.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/template_capabilities.py) | [AppModel+Reasoning.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/AppModel+Reasoning.swift) | Partial | TS renders templates in the Rust engine and probes what the checkpoint template can express (reasoning efforts), but ships no bundled template overrides for stale GGUF templates and no tool-support probe before offering tools. |
| [Native tool token preservation](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/native_tool_tokens.py): keep tool-control tokens alive in decoded output so the parser can tell native calls from markerless prose | [native_tool_tokens.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/native_tool_tokens.py) | none | Missing | Implementing it means an engine-side (Rust) decode allowlist plus Swift surfacing of native-vs-prose provenance so ToolCallParser can trust native envelopes. |
| [Compaction as an event with standing-instruction record](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/checkpoint.py): on overflow, reset to system plus a deterministic verbatim record of standing instructions instead of slope trimming, gated on archived searchable history | [checkpoint.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/checkpoint.py) | [AppChatCompaction.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/AppChatCompaction.swift) | Partial | TS compaction is model-summarized with the full transcript retained, but it has no deterministic verbatim standing-instruction block in the system message and no search-the-archive gate; adding one would survive summarizer failures. |
| [Rolling context window and refusal diagnosis](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/context_window.py): message-aware trimming below budget with headroom, plus threading the shape of an over-size refusal to the user-facing message | [context_window.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/context_window.py), [context_refusal.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/context_refusal.py) | [AppContextUsage.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/AppContextUsage.swift) | Partial | TS has exact context accounting and auto-compaction before the fit, but a failure that still slips through surfaces a generic error; blaming the sent turn vs the irreducible conversation in the message is the gap. |
| [Standing instruction pinning](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/instruction_pin.py): protect the oldest user instruction groups from eviction under an absolute token ceiling so filler follow-ups cannot drop standing rules | [instruction_pin.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/instruction_pin.py) | none | Missing | Pinning protected groups inside AppModel+History prompt assembly (with a token ceiling surfaced in settings) would keep standing rules alive after compaction and filler turns. |
| [Context sizing and memory estimation](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/runtime_context.py): resolved window ceilings, one canonical memory estimate, and a free-VRAM probe for backend fit decisions | [runtime_context.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/runtime_context.py), [memory_contract.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/memory_contract.py), [_vulkan_probe.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/_vulkan_probe.py) | [InstalledModelMemoryFitCardView.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Installation/InstalledModelMemoryFitCardView.swift), [ContextLadderView.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Installation/ContextLadderView.swift) | Full | TS renders engine-computed memory fit, working set and the per-window cost ladder (Metal side, no Vulkan probe subprocess); the difference is where the numbers are computed, not whether users see them. |
| [Default models, clean ids, local resolution](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/defaults.py): curated default model lists per platform, stable public model ids, and resolving request model strings onto downloaded local models | [defaults.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/defaults.py), [model_ids.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/model_ids.py), [local_model_resolver.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/local_model_resolver.py) | [AppModel+ModelDiscovery.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/AppModel+ModelDiscovery.swift), [LocalModelDefaultsSectionView.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Components/LocalModelDefaultsSectionView.swift) | Partial | TS has curated defaults, catalog scans and aliases, but no per-request resolver that maps an arbitrary model string onto an already-downloaded local model for drop-in OpenAI compatibility. |
| [Message content normalization](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/message_content.py): collapse string or OpenAI multimodal list content to plain text safely for formatting and parsing paths | [message_content.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/message_content.py) | [AppModel+History.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/AppModel+History.swift) | Full | TS assembles structured turns with text plus imagePaths natively rather than normalizing list content, and has no pasted_text tag to unwrap since pastes stay inline. |
| [Sampling penalties and repetition guard](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/presence_penalty.py): OpenAI-style presence-penalty logits processor and withholding the length-continuation nudge when the fragment is a repetition loop | [presence_penalty.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/presence_penalty.py), [repetition_guard.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/repetition_guard.py) | [AppModel+Sampling.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/AppModel+Sampling.swift) | Partial | TS has temperature, top-k/p, seed and repetition penalty, but no presence-penalty knob and no auto-continuation path, so there is nothing guarding a continuation against echo loops. |
| [Prefill and decode timing](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/generation_timing.py): device-synchronized prefill/decode split so prompt and generation throughput read truthfully | [generation_timing.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/generation_timing.py) | [AppModel+Generation.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/State/AppModel+Generation.swift), [ServerConsoleView.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Server/ServerConsoleView.swift) | Full | TS reads prefill progress, prompt tokens and tok/s rate straight from engine telemetry; unsloth measures the split itself only because transformers reports no timings. |
| [Mid-stream error diagnosis](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/stream_errors.py): turn a mid-stream engine error chunk into actionable advice, distinguishing KV exhaustion from concurrency starvation and oversized prompts | [stream_errors.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/stream_errors.py) | [ErrorBanner.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Components/ErrorBanner.swift), [ToastOverlayView.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Components/ToastOverlayView.swift) | Partial | TS surfaces generation failures via banner and toast, but there is no mapping of engine error text into distinct remedies; classifying context-family errors would stop users shortening conversations that were not the problem. |
| [SSE control-frame sanitization](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/sse_control_frames.py): strip forged tool-control frames from provider-relayed streams so a remote endpoint cannot fake tool cards | [sse_control_frames.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/sse_control_frames.py) | none | Missing | TS relays no third-party provider streams, so there is nothing to sanitize; it becomes relevant only if the app ever proxies external SSE into its own tool-card protocol. |
| [Transcription streaming and gallery](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/transcript_stream.py): speech-to-text screen with progress streaming and durable account-scoped transcript history | [transcript_stream.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/transcript_stream.py), [transcript_gallery.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/transcript_gallery.py) | [PromptComposerControls.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Generation/PromptComposerControls.swift) | Missing | TS only offers macOS dictation into the composer; a real feature means a local STT sidecar or Speech framework pipeline plus a persisted transcript store akin to the chat rows. |
| [Text tool call parsing and healing](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/tool_call_parser.py): backend-neutral parsing of a dozen family tool-call envelopes plus response-side promotion of text tool calls matching declared tools | [tool_call_parser.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/tool_call_parser.py), [passthrough_healing.py](../../unsloth/studio/backend/core/inference/passthrough_healing.py) | [ToolCallParser.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Tools/Core/ToolCallParser.swift), [ForgeGuardrailsEngine.swift](../swift/TurboSparkApp/Sources/TurboSparkApp/Tools/Guardrails/ForgeGuardrailsEngine.swift) | Partial | TS parses XML, markdown and JSON blocks and its guardrails engine rescues calls standard parsing missed, but it covers a fraction of unsloth's per-family envelopes (Gemma, Mistral, GLM, Kimi, DeepSeek forms); porting more envelopes would widen small-model tool reliability. |
